## Milestones

### M0 · Foundation — done
- [x] Shared core, offline-first sync with conflicts, live sync, AWS backend with CI/CD
- [x] Hardware-bound device keys, signed requests, reconnect after sign-out
- [x] Cable-free installs on the Fold

### M1 · Run my day
- [x] Google Calendar (read-only), FC Barcelona fixtures, day planner v1
- [x] Theme: Dark default, Light, Auto, animated blend (both apps). *Landed 2026-10-05; rule 8 keeps every later screen checked in both themes.*
- [x] Motion foundation: stagger, count-up, skeleton shimmer, pane spring, haptics helpers (`MekaMotionKit` on both apps, `choreography` tokens); applied to Today (intro stagger, up-next cross-slide, complete haptic), Plan (cascade, count-up, skeleton) and Calendars (skeleton, stagger). *Landed 2026-10-05. Shared-element transitions (Plan → Today, list → detail) moved to the App shell item, where navigation lands.*
- [x] App shell: Fold bottom bar + two-pane when unfolded; Mac sidebar (Today · Needs you · Lists · Goals · Review · Vault). Includes shared-element transitions (Plan Apply → Today, list → detail) with `SharedTransitionLayout` / `matchedGeometryEffect`
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-05 (CI green 2026-10-05, run 36, all four jobs):* `ShellNav` rules (unit-tested on both apps, rule for rule); Fold: bottom bar with five destinations when closed, rail with all six when open (Today and Needs you keep their two panes), bar steps aside for the keyboard, each destination keeps its state; Mac: `NavigationSplitView` sidebar with ⌘1–⌘6 in a Go menu; real Needs you screen on both apps with a calm count badge; Lists, Goals, Review and Vault show what lands there. Motion: content slides the way you moved, lit pill springs, tick haptic; reduced motion cross-fades.
  - *Slice 2, 2026-10-06:* `SharedMotion` rules (unit-tested on both apps). Fold: Today and Needs you sit in a `SharedTransitionLayout`; on the closed Fold a task's title travels from its row into the detail pane and back; Plan Apply sends each planned block's title into its row in Today, which is then softly lit for 1.2 s; opening the Fold grows the detail pane out beside the list (and folding shrinks it away) while the list keeps its scroll position. Mac (simpler, as rule 7 allows; the plan is a sheet, so titles can't fly out of it): the selection highlight glides between rows (`matchedGeometryEffect`), the detail slides across to the new task, and planned tasks glide into place and are lit for 1.2 s. Reduced motion: no travel, cross-fades only.
  - *Check on the Fold:* the title flights and the unfold morph can only be judged on the device; adjust timings in `MekaShared.kt` if anything feels slow.
- [x] Work mode + "while you were at work" summary (approved 2026-10-06; #10 approved 2026-10-07): work hours from a schedule plus a manual Work switch; Android notification listener captures WhatsApp, SMS and missed calls during work mode (official notification access only, no WhatsApp libraries); after work, one screen grouped by person with urgent items first ("urgent"/"emergency" breaks through immediately). Summaries are non-AI (grouping, ordering, counts) until the AI layer lands, then one line per person. Never marks anything read, never replies. Family list and an "always ring/always notify" list chosen from contacts. **Answers 2026-10-07:** work hours Mon–Fri 09:00–17:30 confirmed, **except UK bank holidays** (England and Wales list from gov.uk `https://www.gov.uk/bank-holidays.json`, fetched by the server weekly, cached on devices; work mode stays off on those days); Mac sync of the after-work summary approved (through Meka's own server, stored like his other data).
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `WorkMode` (schedule incl. night shifts, a Work switch that lasts until the schedule next changes, max 16 h, synced as the `context_mode/work` entity) and `AfterWorkSummaries` (grouped by person: urgent first, then family, then latest; whole-word "urgent"/"emergency"; re-posted notifications de-duplicated; phone-number formats matched), unit-tested; `MekaCore.workMode` + switch/schedule commands, re-evaluated every 30 s. Fold: `WorkCaptureService` (official notification access) reads WhatsApp, the SMS apps and missed calls only while at work and keeps them in a Keystore-sealed file on the phone (never synced, dropped after 7 days); urgent messages and the always-notify list post a high-importance "Urgent while at work" alert; Work screen (switch, hours, notification access, alerts, Family and Always-notify picked from contacts); Needs you shows "N held for later" during work and the "While you were at work" card after, opening one screen grouped by person with an expandable thread; Done clears MEKA's copy only. Mac: "At work / Off work" in Today opens the Work sheet (switch and hours). Default hours Mon–Fri 09:00–17:30 until changed.
  - *Slice 2, 2026-10-06 (Fold only; the summary itself is Fold-only until #10):* core `AfterWorkNudge` (nudge once on the change from at work to off work, only when something is held and the app isn't on screen; "Ada, Mum and 3 others · 1 urgent · 5 messages · 1 missed call"), unit-tested. Fold: `AfterWorkNudger` checks every work-mode change (clock tick, background sync — so a switch flipped on the Mac counts — the listener, process start) and records the last state, so it nudges once even if the phone was off when work ended; an inexact `setWindow` alarm (10-min window, no exact-alarm permission, ADR-007) wakes it at the end of work. One quiet "After-work summary" channel; names stay off the lock screen ("Your after-work summary is ready"). Tapping opens Needs you with the summary, which clears the nudge.
  - *Slice 3, 2026-10-07 (Mac checked nightly):* UK bank holidays. Server: `GovUkBankHolidays` (backend/integrations/Holidays.kt; GOV.UK's public `bank-holidays.json`, England and Wales, no key, nothing sent) behind a `HolidayProvider` interface; every household follows it like the fixtures and news feeds (a "GOV.UK · England and Wales" row, "Bank holidays · synced …", on the Calendars screen of both apps); refreshed weekly (retried at the next 5-minute poll after a failure; an unreadable or empty list leaves the last one in place), written into one `context_mode/bank_holidays` entity only when it changed (from 1 January last year on), which then wakes the devices (ADR-008 addendum). Core: `BankHolidays`/`HolidayCalendar`/`BankHolidayStore` and date-aware `WorkModeRules` (`scheduledOn`, `isWorkDay`): a shift starting on a bank holiday doesn't count (a night shift belongs to its start day), "next shift" skips holidays ("Off work · Christmas Day · next shift Tue 09:00"; more than a week away "Thu 14 Jan 09:00"), the Work switch still overrides; the brief and the shutdown's tomorrow preview say "Christmas Day · no work" and the evening shutdown starts at 18:00 that day. Everything that reads work mode (the Fold's capture, the after-work nudge, the bedside clock, both apps' Work lines) follows through `MekaCore`. Unit-tested (BankHolidaysTest incl. two-device sync, MekaCoreTest, backend HolidaysTest). CI green (run 37691566516; macOS job at the next nightly run; the core and server were touched, so the server redeploys and starts mirroring the list at its next poll).
  - *Slice 4, 2026-10-07 (Mac checked nightly):* the after-work summary on the Mac (Needs Meka #10). Core: a new synced `held_message` entity (ADR-008 addendum, additive): `HeldMessages.hold/items/summary/clear`, id from the capture's own id (a re-post, a second hold or a reinstall is one entity), the family flag travels with each item (the lists stay on the Fold), Done on either device clears everything held up to then on both (`cleared` TrueWins) and blanks the text, a cleared one never comes back, 7-day window; `MekaCore.afterWork`, `holdCaptured`, `clearAfterWork`; `AfterWorkSummary.itemCount/withLists`. Unit-tested (HeldMessagesTest: the Mac sees the Fold's grouping, re-posts, Done on the Mac, Done on both offline with a later message, the week window; MekaCoreTest across two cores). Fold: the listener also holds each new item in the synced summary (and once at start, for what was held before); Needs you's card, the summary pane and the nudge read the synced summary; Done clears both ("Done clears MEKA's copy on the Fold and the Mac"). Mac: Needs you shows "While you were at work" (or "At work · 12 held for later") above the stack; clicking opens a sheet grouped by person (urgent, family, latest; a person unfolds everything they sent with the expand spring and a tick haptic; Done with a light haptic clears both); the Work sheet's note says the summary shows here too. CI green (run 37695006785; macOS job at the next nightly run; the core was touched, so the server redeploys).
  - *Later:* one AI line per person once the AI layer's first slice lands. Family/always-ring calls belong to the call assistant item.
  - *Check on the Fold:* grant notification access in the Work screen (Android may first need App info → ⋮ → Allow restricted settings, as the app is sideloaded), allow alerts and let the "Urgent while at work" channel through Do Not Disturb. Missed-call and SMS layouts vary by Samsung build: if a sender shows wrongly, the title/text rules are in `NotificationRules.kt`.
- [~] Call assistant for mobile calls at work (Needs Meka #9; code done, waiting on Needs Meka #13): during work mode, MEKA's call screening lets family, the always-ring list and repeat callers (2nd call within 3 min) ring; other calls are declined, and the carrier's "forward when busy" sends them to a cloud phone number where a voice assistant says it is Meka's assistant, that Meka is at work and will call back, takes a message, and asks if it's urgent. Messages land in the after-work summary; urgent ones alert Meka immediately. Fixed greeting written by Meka; the assistant always says it is an automated assistant; one switch turns it off. **Approved 2026-10-07** (paid phone service OK; greeting = "Hi, you've reached Meka's assistant. Meka is at work right now and will call you back. Can I take a message, and is it urgent?"). Provider: Twilio (UK number + voice). Build and test everything behind a provider interface first (call screening on the Fold, webhook + voice flow on the server, messages into the after-work summary, urgent → Critical alert); then add Needs Meka #13 for Meka to open the Twilio account, buy the UK number and set "forward when busy".
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-07 (core touched, so Mac checked nightly):* call screening on the Fold and the one switch. Core: `CallScreeningRules` (non-AI, pure; core/domain/CallScreening.kt): with the switch on and at work, family and the always-notify list (matched by phone number in any format) and repeat callers (a second call within 3 minutes, withheld numbers included) ring; everyone else is declined; off work or switched off every call rings. `PeopleLists` keeps each name's numbers (`nameForNumber`, `hasNumber`, `withNumber`, `pruned`). The switch is a new `callAssistant` field on `context_mode/work` (ADR-008 addendum, additive, LWW, off until Meka turns it on) carried on `WorkModeState.callAssistant`; `MekaCore.setCallAssistant`. Unit-tested (CallScreeningTest incl. two-device sync of the switch). Fold: `MekaCallScreeningService` (Android's call-screening role, asked for in the Work screen with "Let MEKA screen calls"): a declined call is rejected as busy (so the carrier's "forward when busy" takes it), still logged and shown as a missed call; anything slow or unexpected rings (3 s budget); the last few declined callers (number keys and times only) in private preferences. The Work screen gains a "Call assistant" section (status line cross-fades, the permission pill unfolds with the expand spring, tick haptic), and contacts are now picked with a phone number (no contacts permission), stored with the lists in the sealed file; names picked earlier say "No number · add again from contacts so their calls ring". Mac: a "Screen calls at work on the Fold" switch with the same status line in the Work sheet. CI green (run 37700864484; macOS job at the next nightly run; the core was touched, so the server redeploys and GitHub publishes the phone build).
  - *Slice 2, 2026-10-08 (core and Mac touched, so Mac checked nightly):* the voice side. **Server** (backend/CallAssistant.kt): a provider-neutral `CallAssistant` flow behind `VoiceProvider` (ADR-009 row) with `TwilioVoice` as the adapter; routes `POST /v1/voice/{provider}/{incoming|recorded|answered|transcribed}`, each refused unless signed by the phone service over the exact public URL and form (`X-Twilio-Signature`; no device or publisher may call them; while the auth token is unset every webhook is refused). The call: if the one switch (`context_mode/work.callAssistant`, read server-side by an indexed latest-op query) is off, or the server has no single household, the call is turned away as busy; otherwise Meka's greeting ("Hi, you've reached Meka's automated assistant. Meka is at work right now and will call you back. Can I take a message, and is it urgent?"), a two-minute recording (beep, # or hang up to finish), then "Is it urgent? Press 1 or say yes…" (keypad or speech, en-GB), then goodbye; nothing recorded → "I didn't hear a message…" and nothing held. The message is written into the synced after-work summary as a server-authored `held_message` of the new kind `VOICE_MESSAGE` (ADR-008 addendum: one new field `urgent`, TrueWins; fixed op ids per call, so retried webhooks write nothing more); the transcript arrives later as its text (not after Done blanked it) and the recording is then deleted from Twilio. Urgent (the answer, or "urgent"/"emergency" in the transcript) → `Push.urgent`: every device woken at once at high FCM priority (still only `{"t":"sync"}`). **Infra:** Secrets Manager `meka-os-dev/voice/twilio` (`{}` until Meka pastes `{"account_sid", "auth_token"}`; read-only grant; about $0.40/month), env `MEKA_VOICE_TWILIO_SECRET`; infra test. **Core:** `CallAssistantScript` (the fixed words), `CallAssistantRules` (held id, caller name incl. withheld/"ANONYMOUS", the urgent answer, transcript tidying, which voice messages alert), `CapturedItem.urgent/isUrgent/displayLine`, summary lines and the nudge count voice messages ("1 voice message · 1 missed call"), and a caller known only by number shows under their listed name on the Fold (ranked as family). **Fold:** an urgent voice message rings through once on "Urgent while at work" ("Voice message · Mum", its transcript or "Left a message with your call assistant and said it's urgent") after the sync the server's wake starts, within an hour of the call; the summary pane shows "Voice message · “…”". **Mac:** the after-work sheet shows the same line. Tests: core CallAssistantTest (both apps, names by number, alerts once, retries, Done), backend CallAssistantTest (the whole call over HTTP, retries, not urgent / no answer / emergency in the words, transcript after Done, switch off / no household / nothing said, signatures, tampering, unset token, recording deletion, high-priority wake), infra test. CI green (run 37702804322; macOS job at the next nightly run; infra and the server changed, so the stack and server redeploy and the phone build is published).
  - *Remaining:* Needs Meka #13 (the Twilio account, number and forwarding), then a live test call. *Check on the Fold:* whether a declined call posts Samsung's missed-call notification (the summary relies on it); if not, the screening service should hold the call itself.
  - *Low-balance guard (Meka, 2026-10-08, auto-recharge OFF by choice):* **built 2026-10-10 (below).** The server reads the Twilio balance daily (`GET /2010-04-01/Accounts/{sid}/Balance.json`, same secret) and MEKA shows a Needs you card "Call assistant credit low · £4.20 left · Top up" (opens twilio.com/console/billing) below £5, and a heads-up below £2. If the account is suspended or the balance is unreadable, the Fold stops declining calls during work (they ring as normal) so callers are never sent to a dead number; Work screen says "Call assistant paused · Twilio credit".
  - *Low-balance guard, 2026-10-10 (server + core + Fold + Mac; core and macos touched, so Mac checked nightly):* **Core** (CallCredit.kt, non-AI, pure; `CallCreditTest`, 9): a server-authored `context_mode/call_credit` entity (ADR-008 addendum: `state` OK · LOW under £5 · VERY_LOW under £2 · PAUSED, `pence`, `currency`, `since`, `reason` EMPTY under 50p · SUSPENDED · UNREADABLE after a day of failed reads) read by `CallCreditStore`; `CallCreditRules.assess` (what the server writes after a check; a failed read keeps what the devices have for 24 h), `card` (Needs you: "Call assistant credit low" · "£4.20 left · top up so callers can leave a message", or "Call assistant paused · Twilio credit" · "Out of credit · £0.12 left · calls ring as usual at work until it's sorted"; Top up / Open Twilio open twilio.com/console/billing, a new `DecisionEffect.OPEN_LINK` and `DecisionKind.CREDIT`; lit and first in the stack under £2 or paused, else after the tasks; only while the assistant is switched on), `notices` (a new governor source **Call assistant credit**, heads-up once when it drops under £2 and once when it pauses), `healthLine` (Health's call assistant row: "On · £4.20 left · top up soon" / "Paused · …"). `CallScreeningRules.decide(paused)`: while paused every call that would have gone to the assistant rings (`CallReason.PAUSED`; the block list still blocks), and `statusLine(paused)` says "Paused · Twilio credit · calls ring as usual until it's topped up"; `WorkModeState.callAssistantPaused`. **Server** (backend/CallCredit.kt): `CreditSource` on `TwilioVoice` (the account's status and `Balance.json`, same secret, nothing new), `CallCreditWatch` writes only the fields that changed, over each field's head, and wakes the devices; `startCreditWatch` 2 minutes after start, then every 6 hours (hourly while reads fail); nothing is read until the account SID and token are in the secret. **Fold:** Needs you's Top up opens the browser; the Work screen's line. **Mac:** the same in `CoreModel.decide` (`.openLink`) and the Work sheet's line; bridge `testCallCreditReachesSwift`. Tests: `CallCreditTest` (core), `CallCreditFacadeTest` (what the server writes reaches screening and Needs you), backend `CallCreditWatchTest` (4: only changes written, a day of failed reads pauses, nothing before setup, Twilio's two reads). Motion: the Call assistant row's line and the card on the Needs you stack (catalogue updated). Commit d0fa5df: CI run 319 (38048249850) green first try; the deploy follows (the server starts reading the credit 2 minutes after it starts). **The call assistant item is done once Meka checks the Fold's missed-call notification (below); nothing else is buildable in it.**
  - *Check on the Fold:* nothing to do while the credit is above £5. Once it is under, Needs you shows "Call assistant credit low · £… left" with Top up (opens Twilio's billing page); Health → Call assistant says the amount. If it ever runs out, the Work screen says "Paused · Twilio credit" and work calls ring as usual until you top up (within 6 hours of topping up it switches back on its own).
- [x] Repeating tasks and routines (daily/weekly/monthly/yearly; "every 2nd Tuesday"; skip/snooze one occurrence)
  - *2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `Recurrence` (RRULE subset: every N days, weekly on chosen days incl. every other week, monthly on a date — the 31st falls on a short month's last day — or on the 1st–4th/last weekday, yearly incl. 29 Feb), `CivilDate` and a platform `LocalCalendar` so times of day survive the clock change; unit-tested. A repeating task is a series of occurrences: Done or Skip creates the next one with an id made from the series and day, so two devices finishing the same one offline end up with one next occurrence; missed ones aren't back-filled (an open one from an earlier day stays, marked "since Mon 5 Oct"). "Tomorrow" snoozes just this occurrence (or any one-off task) out of Today and the planner until its day. Steps on a repeating task make it a routine: each new occurrence brings them back unticked. Fold: Repeat row in the task detail unfolds the picker (Every day · Every weekday · Every Tue · Every 2 weeks on Tue · Monthly on the 6th · Monthly on the 1st Tue · Every year on 6 Oct), steps with spring ticks, and Done · Skip · Tomorrow · Delete; rows show "↻ Every weekday". Mac: the same as a Repeat menu, steps list and buttons.
  - *Later, if wanted:* a custom rule editor (e.g. "every 3 days" can be stored and shown, but the picker offers presets only); "after completion" repeats (next one counted from the day you finish).
- [x] Capture from anywhere: Android share sheet, home-screen widget, quick-settings tile, voice (on-device speech); Mac menu bar (exists) + Services
  - *2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `QuickCapture` (non-AI: first line the title, the rest kept in the notes; a bare link becomes "Open bbc.co.uk" with the link kept; a share's subject becomes the title; long titles cut at a word with the whole text in the notes), unit-tested; `MekaCore.capture(text, subject)` used by every entry point incl. the in-app capture fields. Shared text is untrusted: it only ever becomes a task's title and notes. Fold: one capture sheet (springs up over whatever is on screen, field focused, "Kept in notes" preview, check pops and the sheet drops away; reduced motion fades) opened by the share sheet, "Capture in MEKA" on selected text, a quick-settings tile and a home-screen widget (pill + mic, follows the phone's light/dark). Voice is the phone's recogniser asked to stay on the device (`EXTRA_PREFER_OFFLINE`, no microphone permission in MEKA). Nothing saves until Add. Mac: Services → "Add to MEKA" on selected text in any app (waits for the core if the request launched the app); the menu-bar and Today fields use the same rule.
  - *Check on the Fold:* add the tile (pull down twice → ✎ → drag "Capture") and the widget (long-press home → Widgets → Meka). If the mic says voice isn't available, install/enable Google's speech services and download the English offline pack (Settings → General management → Voice input). On the Mac, "Add to MEKA" may need ticking once in System Settings → Keyboard → Keyboard Shortcuts → Services → Text.
- [x] Lists: Waiting for (with chase dates), Someday (with kinds), Decisions (with review dates)
  - *2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `Lists` (non-AI). Waiting for is a `commitment` owed to you: what, from whom, a chase date (presets Tomorrow · In 3 days · Next week · In 2 weeks · No date; default 3 days); "Chased" records it and sets the next chase 3 days on; "Got it" closes it (Done wins over a chase made at the same time on the other device). Someday is a task with lifecycle SOMEDAY grouped by kind (Ideas · To buy · Projects · Trips · To read · To look into · Applications · Home · Other), never in Today or the planner; "Do it now" puts it back in Today, and a one-off task's detail has "Someday" to send it there (repeating tasks can't). Decisions: what you decided, why, an optional review date (a month · 3 months · 6 months · a year); when due: "Still right" with the next review, Revisit, or Replace (the old one is kept as superseded, so it isn't re-made). Chase and review dates are days (stored as 09:00 local). Due chases and reviews show as one "From your lists" card in Needs you and count in its badge. Unit-tested incl. two-device sync. Fold: Lists tab with a springing tab pill, rows that unfold their actions, date chips and add fields. Mac: the same with a segmented control and menus.
  - *Later, if wanted:* pick "from whom" from contacts / People (V1); a received item asking to become a task; showing superseded decisions as history; "promises spotted in email → Waiting for" is V1.
- [x] Goals and habits (progress, streaks, planner makes room for habits that are behind)
  - *2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `Goals` (non-AI), unit-tested incl. two-device sync. Habits: how often (every day … once a week, Monday–Sunday weeks), when (morning · afternoon · evening · any time), how long; tick or untick today (a tick on the Fold and the Mac the same day is one tick). Pace: behind / to do today / on track / week done, pro rata in the week a habit is added; streaks count days for a daily habit and weeks that met the target otherwise. Goals: what done looks like, a horizon (weeks · months · years), progress counted from linked habits and tasks (or set by hand in 10 % steps while nothing is linked), Finished keeps it out of the list. Plan my day makes room first for habits that are behind or due, in their part of the day ("↻ Stretch · behind"); habit blocks are shown, not applied. Fold: Goals tab (tick circle pops with a spring and a light haptic, streak number rolls, week dots, progress bars fill on appear, rows unfold their settings) and a Goal row in the task detail. Mac: the same screen with menus, Goal menu in the task detail, habit blocks in the plan sheet.
  - *Later, if wanted:* ticking a habit for an earlier day from the screen (the core allows it); habits in Today; auto-ticks from Health Connect (V1).
- [x] Fasting tracker (timer, eating window, planner-aware, weekly history)
  - *2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `Fasting` (non-AI, no health claims), unit-tested incl. two-device sync. A fast is a `fast` entity (start, goal in hours, end); you start it now or up to 3 h back ("I started an hour ago"), end it, nudge the start (−1 h · −30 min · +30 min), change the goal (12–24 h), undo "End fast" for 10 minutes, or discard a mistaken one. Plan presets 14:10 · 16:8 · 18:6 · 20:4 set the usual goal and the eating window (16:8, eating 12:00–20:00 until changed); the card says "Eating window open until 20:00" / "opens at 12:00" / "closed at 20:00". Two devices starting offline: the earlier start counts, ending ends both, history merges overlaps. History: the last 7 days (each fast on the day it ended) and "5 fasts in 7 days · average 16 h 12 m · 4 reached the goal". Plan my day keeps meals free ("◐ Break your fast" at the goal when it lands today, "◐ Last meal before your fast" before the window closes); nothing is planned over them, and they are shown, not applied. Fold: a Fasting card at the top of Goals (ring sweeps continuously with a one-second timer, soft pulsing glow at the goal, light haptic on start/end, chips to start/adjust/choose a plan, 7 day bars fill on appear). Mac: the same section with menus (Start a fast clicks to start now), `TimelineView` timer, bars and meal rows in the plan sheet.
  - *Later, if wanted:* goal-reached / 16·18·24 h nudges on the Fold (windowed alarms per ADR-007) with the notification governor; a fasting line in Today; custom eating-window times (presets only for now); editing or deleting past fasts.
- [x] Evening shutdown (tick, carry over, tomorrow preview)
  - *2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `EveningShutdown` (non-AI), unit-tested incl. two-device sync. Offered from the end of work on a work day (when work ends 15:00–22:00), else 18:00, while you're not at work and until you shut down; shutting down on either device puts the card away on both until the next evening (stored as the `shutdown` context-mode entity, ADR-008). The screen: "4 done today"; left from today (what Today shows plus plans from earlier days never done; planned first, then overdue, then the rest), each with a tick, Tomorrow (the same snooze as the task detail: times of day kept), Skip (repeating) or Someday (one-off), and "Move the rest to tomorrow"; then Tomorrow · Wed 7 Oct: work hours, all-day events, events and planned tasks in time order (an event still running from tonight shows "Until 01:00"), tasks with no time, and "2 events · 4 tasks · first at 09:30". Fold: a "Shut down the day" card in Today in the evening ("3 left · tomorrow: 2 events, first at 09:30"), a Shut down link in Today's header any time, and the shutdown pane; after it, one quiet line ("Day shut down at 18:42 · Tomorrow: …") reopens it. Mac: the same card, header button and a sheet.

- [x] Renewals and bills radar, manual entry (Obligation entity: MOT, insurance, boiler, subscriptions, cancel-by dates)
  - *2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `Renewals` (non-AI) over the `obligation` entity, unit-tested incl. two-device sync. Kinds: MOT · Car tax · Insurance · Boiler service · Subscription · Bill · Licence or passport · Warranty · Other, each with a default lead time (MOT and boiler 4 weeks, insurance 3, car tax and warranty 2, licence/passport 2 months, subscriptions and bills 3 days; changeable) and a default repeat (yearly, monthly, or none). A due date (any day within ten years), repeat (doesn't repeat · every month · every 3 months · every year, anchored on the due date; the 31st stays the 31st after a short month), cost in pounds (typed, e.g. "9.99"; a bad one is refused with a message) and a cancel-by date (the day before … 30 days before). "Renewed" / "Paid" / "Serviced" / "MOT done" rolls a repeating one to its next date (the cancel-by date keeps its distance; doing it on both devices offline moves it on once, no conflict) and closes a one-off; "Cancelled it" / "Stop tracking" takes it off the radar (kept as cancelled). The radar: Needs doing (overdue, cancel-by within a week, inside the lead time), Coming up (about 3 months), Later; "renews Thu 12 Nov · cancel by Thu 5 Nov · £412 a year"; repeating costs add up to "£60.99 a month · £731.88 a year". What needs doing counts in the Lists line, the Needs you "From your lists" card and its badge ("1 renewal due · 1 to cancel or keep"). Fold: a fourth Lists tab, Renewals (counts beside tab labels only when there's room), rows unfold chips for repeat, lead time, cancel-by and kind, a cost field and a date button (the system date picker in MEKA's colours). Mac: the same tab with menus and a date field.
  - *Later, if wanted:* a reminder notification at the lead time (notification governor item); linking to the car/home/person it's for once People and the Vault land (free-text "for" is stored but not yet editable in the apps); other currencies (GBP only); renewals in the morning brief; letters and emails filling the radar (V1).
- [x] Notification governor v1: tiers, quiet hours, digest at 12:30 and 18:00 (local notifications)
  - *2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `Governor` (non-AI), unit-tested incl. two-device sync. Tiers (ADR-013): Critical posts at once even in quiet hours; Needs a decision posts at once or when quiet hours end; Heads-up posts at its time, but one that falls in quiet hours rides in the next digest instead (with digests off, it posts when quiet hours end) and a stale one is dropped; Digest is summed up at 12:30 and 18:00 ("Evening digest · 3 things", one line each, "1 renewal due · 2 to chase"); App only never posts. A digest missed by more than 2 h is skipped; a heads-up due at digest time rides in it; more than 3 heads-ups at once fold into one ("4 things need you"). `NoticeSources` derives notices on every evaluation, nothing stored: cancel-by dates (heads-up at 09:00 the day before), fasting goal reached (heads-up at the goal, stale after 3 h), "Shut down the day" (heads-up when the evening starts, not once shut down), and in the digest renewals and bills due, things to chase, decisions to review and overdue tasks. Settings sync (`context_mode/quiet`: quiet hours default 22:00–07:00, each digest on/off, any source lowered to Digest or App only, never raised); what this device posts (Everything · Digests only · Off) and what it has posted stay on the device. Fold: one channel per tier (Critical, Needs a decision, Heads-ups, Digests; titles off the lock screen), an inexact `setWindow` alarm for the next digest, the end of quiet hours or the next heads-up (ADR-007, no exact alarms), re-armed after a reboot or update; it looks again whenever lists, fasting, the shutdown, Today or the settings change; tapping opens the right tab (a digest opens Needs you or Lists). Notifications pane from Today's header (chips, quiet-hour steppers unfold, digest toggles, "what goes where" per source, preview lines "Quiet until 07:00 · Next digest 18:00 · 3 things so far"). Mac: the same sheet from Today; this Mac is Off until turned on there (then macOS asks once); posts with macOS notifications (digests passive), checked every 30 s.
  - *Check on the Fold:* open Today → Notifications and allow notifications; let the "Heads-ups" channel make a sound if you want one (Settings → Notifications → Meka). Each tier is its own channel, so Do Not Disturb can let "Critical" through.
  - *Later, if wanted:* the interruptions count for the weekly review (ADR-013); moving the work-mode alerts and the after-work nudge onto the governor's channels and quiet hours; choosing digest times (12:30 and 18:00 can be turned off, not moved); renewal "due today" heads-ups; Mac notification taps opening the right section.
- [x] Push notifications via Firebase — project `meka-os-5ae41` created 2026-10-07; `android/app/google-services.json` committed. Server sends through FCM HTTP v1 with the service account in Secrets Manager `meka-os-dev/fcm/service-account` (service account pasted by Meka 2026-10-07; empty `{}` = push off, keep local notifications as the fallback).
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-07 (Fold and server; the Mac has no push until Needs Meka #6, its long-poll keeps it current while open; core touched, so Mac checked nightly):* "sync now" wake-ups (ADR-004 amendment). Server: `Push` + `FcmSender` (backend/Push.kt, Fcm.kt): when a device stores ops, the household's other devices are sent one data-only FCM message `{"t":"sync"}` (normal priority, collapse key `sync`, TTL 1 h; nothing about Meka's data goes to Google), at most one every 20 s per device with one trailing wake at the end of the gap; sent off the request. The service account is read from Secrets Manager at use time (5-min cache, `{}` = off, refused unless it's a `service_account` for Google's own token endpoint); the RS256 JWT-bearer assertion is signed with the JDK, the access token cached; a token FCM calls unregistered is forgotten. `POST /v1/push/token` (signed, keyed devices only; empty token removes it) into `push_token` (V6 migration, additive; revoked devices never woken). Wire: `WireCodec.PushToken` (shape-checked). Core: `PushApi` on the HTTP transport, `MekaCore.registerPushToken` (false = try later, e.g. a server without push). Unit-tested: wire, transport, the coalescing, gone tokens, other households, the JWT (verified against the key), the FCM request and token reuse, the Postgres store. Fold: Firebase Messaging 24.1.0 (FCM only, no Analytics; analytics/ad-id collection meta-data off), configured from `res/values/firebase.xml` (the google-services plugin's values, checked against google-services.json by `PushTest`, no extra Gradle plugin); `push/MekaMessagingService` runs `SyncWorker.syncSoon` (expedited one-off, network-constrained) on a wake, so Today, the widgets, ongoing notifications and the governor follow a Mac edit within seconds; the token is sent once per new token (on start, after connecting, and retried at each background sync until the server has it); nothing is shown for a wake. CI green (run 37688171086; macOS job at the next nightly run; the core and server were touched, so the server redeploys and applies V6).
  - *Slice 2, 2026-10-07 (Mac checked nightly):* server-side changes wake the devices, and a moved kick-off is announced (ADR-007's server-originated case). Server: a calendar or fixtures poll that wrote ops calls `Push.serverChanged`, which wakes every device of the household (same 20 s coalescing; headline refreshes never wake), so a moved meeting's reminder re-arms and the governor sees changes within seconds of the 5-minute poll. When the fixtures feed moves a timed match whose old kick-off was still ahead, the server writes `movedFromMs`/`movedAtMs` on it (additive, fixtures only; ADR-008 addendum). Core: `FixtureMoves` (pure) and a new governor source, **Kick-off changes** (Heads-up, can be lowered to Digest or App only; listed in Notifications on both apps): "Kick-off moved: Barça v Real Madrid" · "Now 21:00 · was 18:30 · Sat 10 Oct" ("Now Sun 11 Oct 18:30 · was Sat 10 Oct 21:00" when the day changed), once per new time, stale after two days or at kick-off; hidden fixtures stay quiet; tapping opens Today. Unit-tested (FixtureMovesTest incl. the governor; FixturesTest: the move recorded and every poll that wrote wakes the household, a quiet poll doesn't; PushTest). Visible on the Fold and the Mac through their governors (no new UI); the wake itself is Fold-only until #6.
  - *Remaining:* approvals as visible pushes come with V1's approvals inbox; APNs for the Mac once #6 lands; "Moved from 18:30" in the event detail, if wanted. Item done (Meka's key is in, #2). CI green (run 37689863689; macOS job at the next nightly run; the core and server were touched, so the server redeploys).
  - *Check on the Fold:* install this build (`tools/publish-fold.sh` on the Mac, Install on the Fold) and open MEKA once so it registers; then with MEKA closed on the Fold, tick a task on the Mac: within a few seconds the Fold's Needs you widget / ongoing notification should catch up. If it doesn't, Play services must be allowed to run MEKA in the background (Settings → Apps → Meka → Battery → Unrestricted helps on Samsung).
- [x] Morning brief: day summary, waiting-on, news headlines from RSS topics (server-side fetch)
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `MorningBrief` (non-AI), unit-tested incl. two-device sync. Offered from the end of quiet hours (07:00 when quiet hours are off or end outside 05:00–10:00) until noon, until "Got it" on either device (the `context_mode/brief` entity, ADR-008). The brief: "Good morning, Meka · Tue 6 Oct · Work 09:00–17:30"; Today in time order (all-day events, then events and planned tasks, then open tasks with no time, overdue ones marked) with "3 events · 5 tasks · first at 09:30"; Waiting on (chases due today first, lit, up to 5, "+2 more in Lists"); On your lists (renewals, bills and cancel-by dates that need doing, decisions due a review); You (habit pace, a running fast). A "Morning brief" heads-up goes out when it starts (new governor source, can be lowered to Digest or App only; stale at noon; taps open Today). Fold: card in Today in the morning, a Brief link in Today's header any time, and the brief pane. Mac: the same card, header button and a sheet.

Backend: `NewsProvider` (ADR-009) with a first adapter, `BbcNewsRss` — BBC News public RSS feeds for Top stories · UK · World · Politics · Business · Technology · Science · Health · Football (no sign-in, no key, nothing about Meka sent; a plain GET from the server). Every household follows it like the fixtures feed (a "Headlines" row on the Calendars screen shows its status). At most hourly it mirrors the newest 4 items of each topic into fixed slots (`headline` entities, one per topic and slot, so the count never grows); a story still in the feed keeps its slot and isn't rewritten, a new one takes a freed slot, leftovers are marked removed. The XML parser refuses DTDs/external entities; only https links are kept, tracking parameters dropped. Core: `News` (non-AI), unit-tested incl. two-device sync: topics chosen in the brief (synced, `context_mode/news`; default Top stories and World; none is allowed), up to 5 headlines from the last 36 h, newest first, the same story in two topics shown once, "BBC News · World · 2 h ago". Headlines are untrusted (ADR-006): shown as plain text only, a link opens in the browser only when it is https. Fold: a Headlines section at the end of the brief, Topics unfolds chips; tapping a headline opens it. Mac: the same section with a Topics menu; clicking opens the browser.

  - *Order and guard-rails (decided 2026-10-07 22:56):* build in slices by value, each shippable: **Slice 1** sources (Barça, Spain, AI, tech) + dedupe + images on the server, and a **News place** (Ask → More → News, and the Fold's unfolded command centre): Barça lane first, then AI, then the rest; the detail sheet; Topics and sources. **Slice 2** the drifting strip: Today (one line, under the header; it fades to a still row after 2 loops each time Today opens, so Today stays calm — tap ▸ to set it moving again; the Appearance switch can make it always move or hide it) and the Mac floating ticker. **Slice 3** the Fold home-screen flipping widget and the Mac widget. **Slice 4** the bedside clock strip. Battery: the strip animates only while visible and the screen is on; images ≤ 30 KB each, fetched once and cached. Google News RSS is a fallback source that may change; if it breaks the Spanish papers still carry Barça. With the AI layer: label Barça items **Confirmed / Rumour** (Spanish press is rumour-heavy) and merge the same story across papers.
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-08 (core and Mac touched, so Mac checked nightly):* sources and the News place. **Server** (backend/integrations/News.kt): `PublicNewsFeeds`, one new account "AI, tech and Barça news" (one row on Calendars) with ten slots per topic: **Barça** Mundo Deportivo + Sport (Barça RSS) + Google News RSS search "FC Barcelona" (en-GB); **Spain football** Google News "selección española de fútbol" (es) + "La Liga" (en-GB); **AI** The Verge AI (Atom) + TechCrunch AI + MIT Technology Review AI + OpenAI news; **Tech news** Hacker News front page via hnrss.org (200+ points). Anthropic publishes no feed, so it isn't included; BBC Sport's Barcelona page was left out (unverified). The parser now reads Atom as well as RSS, keeps each item's publisher (Google News's " - Marca" title suffix moves into the source), keeps the feed's summary as plain text (≤ 400 chars; none from Google News or Hacker News, whose descriptions are links/points), and keeps meaningful query parameters while dropping tracking ones (utm_*, at_*, oc, click ids). Feeds are **verified at every refresh**: a feed that fails or has nothing usable is skipped and the topic fails (the row says "Couldn't sync") only when none of its feeds could be read. The container can't reach the feeds, so they were verified against saved samples of each format, not live: check the Calendars row after the deploy. **Core:** topics `barca`, `ai`, `tech`, `spain` (defaults Barça · AI · Top stories · World; chips lead with Barça and AI); a choice made before these existed gets Barça and AI switched on once (new synced `newsTopicsKnown`), and turning them off sticks; `headline.summary` (ADR-008 addendum); `NewsRules.storyKey` (case, accents and punctuation ignored) so the same story from two papers shows once, in the brief too; `NewsPlace` (`MekaCore.newsPlace`): lanes Barça, AI, then the other chosen topics, each story once across lanes, two days, ten per lane, "From Mundo Deportivo, Sport and Google News", the detail with "3 of 18" and Previous/Next. Unit-tested (core NewsTest, MekaCoreTest, backend NewsTest on saved samples incl. Atom, Google News and a feed failing; Mac bridge test). **Fold:** Ask → More → **News** (after Morning brief): the row's label travels into the title, Topics unfolds the chips (tick haptic) with the sources named, lanes stagger in, Barça stories carry an accent dot; tapping a story springs the detail up (summary, "Read full story" opens the browser, Previous/Next or swipe sideways, the story slides the way you moved). **Mac:** the same row opens a News sheet: Topics menu, lanes, click pushes the detail across (← → step, Esc back). Tests: core NewsTest (matchday lines, live, final whistle, TBC, not tomorrow/another calendar/all-day, two in a day, only with Barça, the facade with and without the fixture), CommandCentreTest (glance order, sizes, nothing → left out, match only), Mac bridge test.   - *Images, 2026-10-08 (core and Mac touched, so Mac checked nightly):* pictures on every story. **Server** (backend/integrations/NewsImages.kt): the parser reads each item's picture (`media:thumbnail` / image `media:content`, the one nearest 320 px wide; an image `enclosure`; Atom `rel="enclosure"`; else the first `<img>` in the description or content), https only. At each hourly refresh `NewsImages` fetches pictures it doesn't have yet (public hosts only, checked on every redirect; image types only; ≤ 5 MB; at most 40 new and 90 s per refresh; WebP isn't asked for), decodes them with the JDK's ImageIO (size checked first; logos and pixels under 64 px refused) and re-encodes a **JPEG ≤ 320 × 320 px and ≤ 30 KB** (JPEG instead of WebP: the JDK can't write WebP and a native encoder isn't worth it at this size). Kept in the server's **own database** (migration V7 `news_image`, additive; shared by every household, deleted 3 days after no feed mentions it; a few MB, so no S3 bucket and no cost). Headlines carry only the server's key (`headline.image`, ADR-008 addendum), never the publisher's address; devices fetch `POST /v1/news/image` (keyed devices only; publisher refused). **Core:** `Headline/NewsItem.imageKey` (anything not a 32-hex key ignored), `NewsItem.tileInitial`, `MekaCore.newsImage` / `newsImageBase64` (Mac) with an in-memory cache (100 pictures, misses not re-asked for 10 min, nothing on disk). **Fold:** each story in News shows its picture on the right (72 × 54, cropped), the detail leads with it (16:9); a tile with the source's initial (Barça's colour on Barça stories) stands in until it arrives, and the picture cross-fades in. **Mac:** the same in the News sheet. Tests: core NewsTest, NewsImagesTest (facade), WireCodecTest, backend NewsImagesTest (making pictures, refusing junk, every way feeds name them, public hosts, keep/tidy, the refresh cap, headlines carrying keys only, the route, Postgres store), Mac bridge test. ADR-009 row. The feeds' real pictures can't be fetched from the build container: check News on the Fold after the deploy (pictures appear from the server's next hourly refresh). CI green (run 37708178873; macOS job at the next nightly run; the core and server were touched, so the server redeploys and GitHub publishes the phone build).

The choice is **per device** (kept with the theme; nothing synced, no ADR change). Ask → More → Appearance unfolds **News ticker** chips (Calm · Always moving · Off, tick haptic, the line cross-fades) and **Topics ›** (opens News). **Mac:** the same strip at the foot of Today above the capture field (hover holds it, click opens the News sheet on the story; reduced motion pages ‹ ›); Appearance gains a News ticker segmented control and Topics…. - [x] Weekly review with north-star metrics (ADR-013)
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `WeeklyReview` (non-AI), unit-tested incl. two-device sync. One Monday–Sunday week: tiles (Done · Habit ticks "of 10" · Fasts · Lists closed) and "3 more done than the week before"; Done (tasks finished in the week, newest first, up to 12 and "and 3 more", with the day); Habits (ticks against the week's target, pro rata in the week one was added, dots for the days, "5 of 7 · met"; habits added after the week left out); Goals (progress now, "2 tasks done this week"); Fasting ("4 fasts · average 16 h 10 m · 3 reached the goal") and lists ("2 things you were waiting for arrived", "1 decision made", "1 renewal or bill dealt with"); Still open (planned or due in the week and not done, lit); the week ahead ("Next week", or "This week" when looking back at last week: events, tasks planned or due, renewals and bills due, decisions to review, chases); and the six north-star metrics, each saying when it starts counting (none can be counted before MEKA acts, reminds or asks for you). Opens on last week Monday–Wednesday, this week from Thursday; ‹ › step back up to 12 weeks (a screen choice, not synced). "Done reviewing" syncs (`context_mode/review`, ADR-008). Fold: the Review tab (was a placeholder). Mac: the Review sidebar section, ⌘[ ⌘] step weeks.
  - *Slice 2, 2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* the Sunday-evening nudge. Core: `ReviewCard` on the review view (the week ending on Sunday, or just ended on Monday; offered from 18:00 Sunday through Monday until that week is reviewed on either device; "Review your week" / "Review last week" with "12 done · 3 of 4 habits met · 2 still open"), unit-tested incl. two-device sync; a new governor source, Weekly review (Heads-up at 18:00 on Sunday, one key per week so it posts once, stale when Monday ends, can be lowered to Digest or App only; at 18:00 it usually rides in the evening digest); a new notice target, Review. Fold: the card rises into Today; tapping it (or the notification) opens the Review tab on that week. Mac: the same card in Today, opening the Review section on that week.
  - *Slice 3, 2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* Interruptions counted. Core: `Interruptions` (non-AI), unit-tested incl. two-device sync: after each governor run the platform reports what actually reached you (`notificationsPosted`); only Critical, Needs a decision and Heads-up count (digests never do); counts only, one `interruption_day` entity per device per day (ADR-008), never text. Reporting even with nothing posted starts the count (`countingSince`), so earlier weeks say "Counted from Tue 6 Oct" rather than 0. The review's Interruptions line: "7" with "5 heads-ups · 2 needed a decision · 3 fewer than the week before" (compared only when the week before was counted in full; "None reached you" for 0). Fold: counts posts to channels that aren't turned off in the phone's settings. Mac: counts what macOS accepted while Meka is allowed to notify (nothing while this Mac is Off). Both apps: a numeric north-star value counts up on appear.

- [x] Search everything (tasks, events, lists, decisions; documents when the Vault lands)
  - *2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `Search` (non-AI, local), unit-tested incl. two-device sync. Searches what is already synced on the device; nothing is sent anywhere, stored or indexed (the data is small enough to scan per keystroke). Every word typed must start a word in the item ("den" finds "Dentist", "boil serv" finds "Boiler service"); case, common accents and apostrophes ignored; at least 2 letters. Titles weigh more than places and people, which weigh more than notes; a title holding the whole phrase comes first. Searched: open tasks (title, notes, steps), calendar events (title, place, calendar; upcoming soonest first, then past), Waiting for (what, from whom, notes), decisions incl. replaced ones (statement, why), renewals and bills (title, what it's for, kind, notes), Someday, goals (title, what done looks like), habits, and done tasks (a routine's done occurrences fold into the latest). Groups in a fixed order (Tasks · Calendar · Waiting for · Decisions · Renewals and bills · Someday · Goals · Habits · Done), 20 per group with "and 5 more · keep typing to narrow"; each result has its line ("Planned today 14:00 · ↻ Every weekday", "Tomorrow · 20:00–22:00 · Camp Nou", "Done yesterday", the list row's own line) and a snippet when the match is in the notes, steps or rationale. Results follow edits and sync while the query is set (`MekaCore.search` / `searchView`). Fold: "Search" first in Today's header opens the search pane: an open task opens its detail over the results, a done one unfolds Reopen, a list item opens Lists on its tab with the row unfolded, a goal or habit opens Goals with the row unfolded, events are shown only. Mac: ⌘F (Edit → Search Everything…) or "Search" in Today opens a sheet with the detail beside the results; the same rules.
  - *Later, if wanted:* documents when the Vault lands; people (V1); searching headlines and held work messages (kept out for now: headlines are untrusted and the held messages are Fold-only); highlighting the matched words.

- [x] Self-updating phone app (checks the server for a newer signed APK; you tap Install once)
  - *2026-10-07 (Mac checked nightly):* the Mac publishes, the Fold offers, Meka taps Install. `tools/publish-fold.sh` (on the Mac that installs MEKA, so the debug signing key matches) builds the APK and opens `mekaos://publish-fold-update?apk=…`; MEKA on the Mac (or File → Publish Fold Update… and pick the APK) shows "MEKA 0.1.412 · build 412 · 24.3 MB", checks it's a MEKA APK with Gradle's `output-metadata.json` beside it, and on Publish uploads it in 1 MiB chunks over the normal signed requests (each chunk retried; what arrived is kept). Server: `/v1/releases/{latest,chunk,upload}` for keyed devices only, per household; a build must be newer than the published one (the same build again is a no-op, the same number with other contents is refused); offered only once every chunk is in and the whole file matched its SHA-256; the newest two builds are kept (V5 migration, additive: `app_release`, `app_release_chunk`; tens of MB in Postgres, no new AWS resources). Every build's versionCode is now the commit count (`-Pmeka.versionCode` overrides), versionName `0.1.N`. Fold: checks on open (at most every 10 min) and with background sync; Today shows "MEKA update ready · Build 412 · 24.3 MB" with Install and Later (Later hides that build only). Install asks once for Android's "Install unknown apps" switch, downloads straight into an Android install session with a filling bar, checks the SHA-256, then Android's own Install prompt (it refuses a build not signed like the installed app); if MEKA was in the background the prompt comes back when it opens. Core: `AppUpdateRules` (domain, pure), `ReleaseTransfer` + `MekaCore.latestRelease/downloadRelease/publishRelease` (facade), wire documents; unit-tested on the server (in memory and Postgres), the facade and the wire. CI green (run 70); server deployed (deploy run 70).

Android's developer verification (ADR-010) will cover these installs when it reaches the UK; the free Limited Distribution account keeps them working.

The export is this device's replica as it stands: every entity type (tasks, steps, waiting for, decisions, renewals and bills, goals, habits and their ticks, fasts and the fasting plan, calendar events, settings such as quiet hours, work hours and the review/brief/shutdown marks, interruption counts, household and people), the winning value of each field and, where two devices disagree and nobody has chosen yet, every value on offer (`conflicts`), so nothing is lost. Deleted entities and BBC headlines (not Meka's data) are left out. One readable JSON file, `meka-export-2026-10-07.json` (`format: meka-os-export`, `version: 1`): values keep their type, every `…AtMs` time has a readable UTC copy beside it (`…At`), stable order so the same data exports byte for byte the same, `documents: []` until the Vault lands. Core: `CommandBarRules` (non-AI, pure, nothing stored), unit-tested: a fixed catalogue of what the apps already do: Go to (Today · Needs you · Calendar · Ask · Lists · Goals and habits · Review · Vault), Do (Plan my day · Morning brief · Shut down the day · Start work / Finish work · Start a fast / End the fast · Sync now), Open (Work mode · Notifications · Activity · Your data · Calendars) and Appearance (Dark · Light · Auto); only the half of each pair that fits now (at work, fasting, the current appearance; Calendars and Sync once connected). Matched like Search (every word typed must start a word; titles count more than the other words people use, so "rev" → Review, "fast" → Start a fast, "exp" → Your data, "theme" → the appearances) from the first letter; at most 8 commands, then "Search for “…”" (from two letters) and "Add “…” as a task" (the capture rule), so the bar is never a dead end; nothing typed lists the catalogue by section. Mac: `Shell/CommandBar.swift`: ⌘K (Edit → Command Bar…) from anywhere in the window opens the bar over a dimmed window; ↑ ↓ move (the lit row's highlight glides), Return runs, Esc or a click outside closes; each row shows its symbol, line and the Mac shortcut for the same thing (⌘1–⌘8, ⌘R, ⇧⌘A, ⇧⌘E, ⌘F). Each action does exactly what its button elsewhere does (`CoreModel.perform`; Plan, Brief and Shut down go to Today and open their sheets; Search for opens the ⌘F sheet with the words). `CommandBarNavTests` (nightly) checks the Go commands match the Go menu. Asking in words comes with the AI layer (#3). CI green (run 37675067976; macOS job at the next nightly run; the core was touched, so the server redeploys). - [x] Fasting v2 (Meka, 2026-10-07: "some weeks I fast for 5 days; just track, remind me and keep the history"): two modes — **daily window** (as built) and **extended fast** (start "5-day fast" or "until Friday 18:00"); a running fast shows days + hours ("Day 3 of 5 · 62 h") on Today and as an ongoing notification; gentle check-ins (once a day, quiet hours respected) and a "you did it" moment with a ring burst at the end; ending early is fine and recorded as is. **History**: every fast with start, end, planned vs actual, streak of completed fasts, a calendar heat strip; export with the rest. Start/stop from Ask ("start a 5-day fast"), the card or the notification. Tracking only: no food, calorie or weight advice.
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-07 (Mac checked nightly):* extended fasts and the history. Core: `Fasting` gains the extended kind (`startExtended(hours)`: 24 h · 36 h · 2 · 3 · 5 · 7 days; `startUntil(untilMs)`: "Until Tue 18:00" … six days ahead, at least 12 h away; goals up to ten days) over two new `fast` fields, `kind` and `goalAtMs` (additive, LWW; ADR-008 addendum). A running extended fast is "5-day fast" / "Fast until Fri 18:00" with "Day 3 of 5 · 62 h" (past the goal "Day 6 · 122 h · goal reached") and "Goal 5 days · Sat 20:00"; a nudge of its start isn't refused for being more than two days back once the fast already is; the planner plans no meals around it until its goal day. `FastingHistory` (derived, nothing stored): every finished fast newest first (30 shown; all kept and exported) with planned against actual ("2 d 02 h of 3 days · ended early" · "16 h 20 m · goal reached") and when ("Mon 5 Oct 20:00 – Tue 6 Oct 12:00"); the streak ("3 fasts in a row reached the goal"); totals ("42 fasts · 31 reached the goal · longest 4 d 18 h"); a twelve-week Monday–Sunday heat strip of hours fasted each day (a running fast counts to now; levels under 8 h · under 16 h · under a day · the whole day). The ongoing notification and the Mac menu bar say "5-day fast · Day 3 of 5" (looked at again when the day turns), the Fast widget "5-day fast" with "Goal at Sat 20:00", the brief "5-day fast · Day 3 of 5 · 62 h"; the weekly review counts an "until" fast against its own goal. Unit-tested incl. two-device sync (domain, ongoing, facade). `MekaCore.startExtendedFast/startFastUntil`. Fold: "Longer fast" chips on the Fasting card (the six lengths, then the six "Until" days), the day line in the accent colour, the ring's "of 5 days", goal chips in days for an extended fast, and History under the week (streak, heat strip, "History" unfolds the rows). Mac: a "Longer fast" menu (lengths, then the "Until" days), the same lines, Goal menu in days, and the history with a disclosure. An extended fast's goal heads-up is now "You did it · 5 days" · "5-day fast · end it whenever you're ready" (daily fasts keep "Fasting goal reached"). `FastNow.doneLine` ("You did it · 5 days" / "You did it · 36 h") once an extended fast reaches its goal. Unit-tested through the governor (FastingTest). - [~] Gym (Meka, 2026-10-07: getting back into it): MEKA's job is getting you there, not programming workouts. A "Gym" habit with a target (e.g. 3×/week) and preferred times; the planner **books the slots** around the calendar (shown in Next up, leave-by reminder); after the slot one tap asks "Went?" (or it ticks itself from a Galaxy Watch workout via Health Connect when that's on); an optional one-line note ("push day", "5 km") and an optional rotating label (Push · Pull · Legs) so you know what's next. Missed sessions are rebooked, not nagged. Detailed workout programming stays in a fitness app; MEKA links to it.
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-08 (core and Mac touched, so Mac checked nightly):* the booking, "Went?" and the rotation. **Core** (core/domain/Sessions.kt, non-AI, pure): `SessionRules.book` books a habit with **Book my sessions** on: from today to Sunday, as many sessions as the week still needs (its target pro rata, less the days ticked), each in the habit's part of the day when there's room (evening 17:00–21:30, morning 06:30–12:00, afternoon 12:00–17:00), else any time 07:00–21:30, clear of timed events (15 min either side to get there and back, an hour before kick-off) and of work hours (15 min after work; bank holidays and weekends free; a night shift keeps its morning), with a rest day between sessions while the week leaves room, and two booked habits never overlapping. Worked out afresh from what's there, so a meeting on the slot moves it and **"Didn't go" or a slot that passed unticked rebooks the remaining sessions** on the days left (never a nag); today's slot is worked out from the whole day, so it stays put and becomes "Did you go?" once over. The **rotation** (No rotation · Push · Pull · Legs · Upper · Lower · Full body A · B) labels sessions in turn from the last one ticked. Today's card: "Today 17:45–18:45" · "Now · until 18:45" · "Did you go? · 17:45–18:45" (Went · Didn't go) · "Went · 2 of 3 this week" with the note and "Next: Thu 17:45 · Pull" · "Not today · no worries" with "Rebooked for Tue 17:45" or "No other slot this week"; Undo. Goals' line "Booked Today 17:45 · Wed 17:45 · Fri 17:45" / "Week done · 3 of 3" / "· no room for 1 more". The planner keeps today's session free and shows it (`DayPlanner.plan(sessions)`); booked habits aren't placed again as flexible ones. Five additive fields (ADR-008 addendum): `habit.bookSlots`, `habit.rotation`, `habit_completion.missed/label/note`. `MekaCore.sessionsView`, `addGym`, `setHabitBooked`, `setHabitRotation`, `sessionWent`, `sessionMissed`, `setSessionNote`, `undoSession`; habits may now be 90 min. Tests: SessionsTest (after work, a meeting and a fixture, bank holidays, ticked days and a met week, did you go, rebooking, a short week, rotation, notes, a habit added late, two habits, night shifts, two devices, the planner), MekaCoreTest (the whole day across two cores), Mac bridge test. **Fold:** Goals → "Add Gym" (3 a week, evenings, an hour, booked) until a booked habit exists; a habit's settings gain "Book my sessions" On/Off and Rotation chips, and its row the booked line in the accent colour; Today shows the session card under Up next (goals/SessionCard.kt; motion catalogue row added). **Mac:** the same: Add Gym, a "Book my sessions" switch and Rotation menu, the line, and the card on Today (Goals/SessionCards.swift) with a note field after Went. - [x] Alarms (Meka, 2026-10-07): **smart wake alarm** — suggests a wake time from tomorrow's first commitment + your prep/commute buffer (you confirm or set your own in the evening shutdown); a real full-screen alarm (Android exact alarm, snooze, gradual volume); after you dismiss it, the morning brief opens (and later reads aloud). **Leave-by alarms** for events with a place (buffer you set; travel times when a maps source is added). **Quick alarms/timers** from capture ("alarm 6:30", "timer 20 min"). Mac: alarms as critical notifications. Motion: the dismiss slider glows, the brief rises in.
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-08 (core and Mac touched, so Mac checked nightly):* the **smart wake alarm**. **Core** (core/domain/Alarms.kt, non-AI, pure, unit-tested): `AlarmRules.firstCommitment` (the first timed event from 04:00 on the wake day, or the start of work on a work day, bank holidays and a manual Work off excluded; events win a tie) and `suggest` (less the get-ready buffer, rounded down to five minutes, never before 04:00; nothing early → no suggestion, "Nothing early tomorrow · set one if you like", 07:00 offered); the wake day is tomorrow, or today before 04:00. `WakeView`: "Tomorrow · Fri 9 Oct", the time, "Suggested from Standup at 08:00 · 1 h to get ready" → "Alarm set · Standup at 08:00, 1 h to get ready"; a set alarm stays put, and when the suggestion moves earlier (a new 07:30 call) it says "Alarm set · Early call at 07:30 now, so 06:30 gives you 1 h" with **Use 06:30**. A new synced `alarm` entity (ADR-008 addendum, additive, LWW; id `wake.d<day>`) and `context_mode/alarms.wakeBufferMin` (30 · 45 · 60 · 75 · 90 · 120 min offered; 15 min–3 h accepted). Snooze 9 min ("Snoozed until 06:54"); Dismiss on either device ends it on both; unanswered it rings 10 minutes and is never rung late. `MekaCore.wakeView / nextAlarm / setWake / useSuggestedWake / wakeOff / setWakeBuffer / snoozeAlarm / dismissAlarm / ringingAlarm`. Tests: AlarmsTest (suggestions, bank holiday, the evening view, after midnight, ring/snooze/dismiss across two devices, ten minutes, set again), MekaCoreTest (BST, two cores), Mac bridge test. **Fold:** the shutdown pane gains **Wake alarm** after Tomorrow (‹ 07:45 ›, Set alarm / Turn off, Use 06:30, "Time to get ready" chips; "May ring up to 5 min late · Allow on time" until Alarms & reminders is allowed). `AlarmScheduler` registers the next alarm with `setAlarmClock` (ADR-007 addendum; else a 5-minute window), re-armed at process start; `AlarmRingService` (media-playback foreground service) plays the phone's alarm sound on the alarm stream, rising from 5 % to full over 30 s (`AlarmRouting.volumeAt`), vibrates, stops when answered on either device or after 10 min; a full-screen notification (Snooze 9 min · Dismiss) opens `AlarmActivity` over the lock screen: the time large, what it's for, Snooze, and **Slide to dismiss** with a glowing, breathing brass thumb; Dismiss asks to unlock and springs the **morning brief** up over Today. AlarmRoutingTest. **Mac:** the shutdown sheet's `WakeSection` (the same controls, the buffer as a menu); `MacAlarm` schedules the next alarm as a notification with Snooze 9 min · Dismiss (they sync; Dismiss opens the brief sheet). A critical/time-sensitive notification needs the Developer Program (#6, skipped), so it's an ordinary one with the default sound. Core (core/domain/QuickAlarms.kt, non-AI, pure): `QuickAlarmRules.parse` reads one typed line — alarms "alarm 6:30" · "alarm at 18:30" · "set an alarm for 7pm" · "wake me up at 7am" (24-hour unless am/pm; the next such time, today or tomorrow; a bare hour only when nothing follows, so "alarm 2 things" stays a task) and timers "timer 20 min" · "timer 1 h 30" · "timer 1h30" · "timer 90 s" · "20 min timer" · "set a timer for 1.5 h" · "timer 25" (1 s–24 h), each optionally with what it's for ("timer 20 min pasta", "alarm 7pm to call Mum"); anything else is a task. **Only text typed into MEKA's own capture field** (Today's capture bar, the Mac's capture field, menu bar and ⌘K "Add task") is read this way: `MekaCore.captureTyped` → `CaptureOutcome` (TaskAdded · AlarmSet with its line · Empty); the share sheet, widget/tile capture sheet and Mac Services still go through `capture` and only ever make tasks (ADR-006). `AlarmKind` gains `ALARM` and `TIMER`; the `alarm` entity gains `atMs` (a timer's exact moment) and `lengthSec` (ADR-008 addendum, additive); `Alarms.setQuick` (an alarm for the same minute is one alarm on both devices), `cancel`, `quickItems`; `AlarmRing.kind` / `opensBrief` (only the wake alarm opens the brief on Dismiss). - [~] Edit your calendars from MEKA (Meka, 2026-10-07: "one shop for my life") — right after Calendar actions. Add, edit, move and delete real Google (and later Outlook) events from both apps.
  - **Permission:** per account, switched on in Calendars ("Allow editing"); reconnecting asks Google for `calendar.events` (Microsoft: `Calendars.ReadWrite`). Read-only accounts keep working. Needs Meka #12 when it lands.
  - **You act, it happens:** your own taps apply straight away (no approval), with a 5-second Undo before the change is sent to Google. Edits made offline queue and send on reconnect; if the event changed in Google meanwhile, show both versions and let Meka choose (never overwrite silently).
  - **Guards:** deleting an event you organise with other guests says "This cancels it for 4 people" and needs a second tap; declining an invite (it notifies the organiser) is always an explicit tap, never automatic; MEKA itself never edits, declines or deletes others' events. Anything MEKA proposes from email or AI (later) goes through Needs you for approval first. Every change is in the activity log.
  - **Add event:** a sheet with title, day, time (or all day), calendar (default remembered), place, notes; natural typing ("Dentist Fri 3pm") fills the fields. Plan my day can also put its blocks in your calendar when you tap Apply (setting, off by default).
  - **Motion:** the sheet springs up, a moved event slides to its new slot in the timeline, a deleted one collapses with the undo bar rising.
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-08 (core and Mac touched, so Mac checked nightly):* **the permission**. Nothing is written to a calendar yet. **Server:** `CalendarProvider.writeScope` (Google `calendar.events`, asked for beside `calendar.readonly`, which still lists the calendars; Microsoft `Calendars.ReadWrite`) is asked for only when the sign-in starts with editing (`POST /v1/integrations/{provider}/connect` now takes a signed `{"editing": true}`; an empty body stays read-only); the callback records `can_edit` only when editing was asked for and the granted scope holds the write scope word for word (`grants`: `calendar.events.readonly` doesn't count), so unticking it leaves the account connected read-only and the browser page says so; a read-only reconnect gives editing up; Microsoft redeems the code and refreshes with the same scopes. `POST /v1/integrations/{provider}/editing` (signed, keyed devices only) takes `{"email", "editing": false}` and stops editing at once (asking for `true` there is refused: allowing needs the provider's consent). Migration V8 (additive): `oauth_state.editing`, `integration_account.can_edit` (existing accounts read-only). The privacy page says MEKA changes a calendar only where editing was allowed. **Core:** `WireCodec.encodeConnectRequest/EditingChange`, `IntegrationAccount.canEdit` ("edit", absent = false); `ConnectedAccount.canEdit/editingLine/editingAction`; `MekaCore.startConnect(provider, editing)` and `stopCalendarEditing`; `CalendarAccessRules` (core/domain/CalendarAccess.kt, non-AI, pure: which accounts offer it — never the feeds —, the lines "Read-only · MEKA only reads this calendar" / "Editing allowed · events you add or change in MEKA go to Google", Allow editing · Stop editing, the header, the note before the browser opens, Reconnect keeping what it had). **Fold:** Calendars shows the editing line under each account (cross-fades) and an **Allow editing** / **Stop editing** action (tick haptic); Allow editing says what Google will ask and opens its page; Reconnect asks for editing again when the account had it. **Mac:** the same in the Calendars sheet (press style, the line cross-fades). Before sending it reads the provider's copy (`CalendarProvider.event`): gone → a delete is done, a change refused; someone else organises it → refused ("MEKA only changes your own events"); changed in what the edit touches → CLASH with their version; a delete with guests needs `guestsOk` (Meka's second tap) and then tells them. Adds are idempotent (Google: our own event id `meka<key>`; Outlook: `transactionId`); changes are PATCHes of only the changed fields. 401 → "Reconnect", 403 → refused, 5xx/timeouts retried 30 s → 30 min apart and given up after a day; the account is re-synced right after so every device shows the change. `event_mirror.remote_id` (migration V9, additive; filled by the next poll, an edit waits for it). - [x] Mac database encryption (spike S6); fix the Mac keychain prompts (no Apple-signed cert: use Secure Enclave-wrapped key files)
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-07 (Mac checked nightly):* the keychain prompts. `SealedKeyFiles` (Mac): the device id, household, enrolment secret, server address and the signing key's enclave handle now live in small files in Application Support/os.meka.mac/keys (folder 0700, files 0600, excluded from Time Machine), each sealed with a P-256 key-agreement key held in the Secure Enclave (its blob only works on this Mac, usable only while it's unlocked): a fresh ephemeral key per write, HKDF-SHA256 bound to the file's name, AES-256-GCM, so a copied, edited or swapped file won't open. Files carry no code-signature ACL, so an ad-hoc build no longer asks for the login keychain password after each rebuild. The first launch moves the old `os.meka.mac.identity.v3` Keychain values across once (all or nothing: if any can't be read, e.g. the prompt was cancelled, nothing is moved and that launch keeps the Keychain, so the device's identity is never lost; old items are left untouched). A Mac without a Secure Enclave keeps the Keychain. Unit-tested (`SealedKeyFilesTests`, with a software key standing in for the enclave). Mac only: the Fold already keeps its keys in the Android Keystore.
  - *Slice 2a, 2026-10-07 (Mac checked nightly):* the SQLCipher linkage (spike S6). The Mac app links Zetetic's official `SQLCipher.swift` package, pinned at 4.19.0 (BSD-style licence, text in docs/third-party-notices.md; free, open source, no service), instead of the system `-lsqlite3`, so MekaKit's SQLiter driver runs on SQLCipher. Without a key SQLCipher reads and writes a plain database exactly like SQLite, so Meka's existing database is untouched and still unkeyed in this slice. `MacDatabase.cipherVersion/opens` and `MacCoreFactory.databaseEncryptionProbe` work on throwaway databases only; `MekaCoreBridgeTests.testDatabaseEngineIsSQLCipher` asserts `PRAGMA cipher_version` is 4.x, that a keyed file has no plain SQLite header, reopens with its key, and is refused with the wrong key or none.
  - *Nightly Mac run green 2026-10-07 (run 37686245413, at bb03541): `testDatabaseEngineIsSQLCipher` passed, so slice 2b can go ahead.* Every "Mac checked nightly" item up to that commit is now checked.
  - *Slice 2b, 2026-10-08 (core and Mac touched, so Mac checked nightly; the Swift was written without a Mac to compile on, so the nightly run is its first build):* **the Mac database is encrypted**. **Core** (core/domain/DatabaseProtection.kt, non-AI, pure): `DatabaseProtectionRules.fileOf` (missing · plain "SQLite format 3" header · sealed), `plan(file, key)` → `DatabasePlan(setAside, encryptFirst, keyed)` (a plain file is encrypted first; a sealed file with no key, or beside a key made just now, is renamed aside, never deleted; without a key nothing is encrypted), `setAsideName` ("meka-set-aside-20261008-1606.db"), `macLine` (encrypted · no Secure Enclave, FileVault only · not yet, tries again next launch), `PHONE_LINE`, `setAsideLine`; DatabaseProtectionTest. `MacCoreFactory.databaseOpensWithKey` opens a file exactly as the core will. **Mac:** `DeviceIdentity.databaseKey()` keeps a 256-bit key as a sealed file only (`database_key`, written and read back before use; no Keychain fallback); `DatabaseEncryption` (macos/MekaOS/DatabaseEncryption.swift, `import SQLCipher`): before the core opens `meka.db`, SQLCipher is checked on throwaway files (the S6 probe), then a plain file is copied with `sqlcipher_export` into `meka.db.encrypting` (user_version carried over), checked (sealed header, the same rows in every table, opens through the core's driver with the key), swapped in (the plain file waits as `meka.db.plain-old` with its WAL/SHM), opened again, and only then the plain file is removed; any failure keeps the plain file and the next launch tries again; a swap interrupted by a quit is put back. `CoreModel` passes the key with `encrypted: true`; a set-aside file shows its one-time line. Your data says how the database is protected (Mac: the line above; Fold: "encrypted (SQLCipher, AES-256) with a key Android's Keystore holds"). ADR-002 addendum. Tests: DatabaseEncryptionTests (a plain database with a task encrypted once with every row kept and unreadable without the key, opened again next launch and by the core; no key / no SQLCipher changes nothing; a sealed file whose key is gone set aside and still opening with its own key; an interrupted swap put back). CI green (run 37808166768; macOS job at the next nightly run; the core was touched, so the server redeploys).
  - *Check on the Mac:* after the next `tools/install-mac.sh`, open MEKA: Today should look as before (nothing lost), and Ask → More → Your data should say "MEKA's database on this Mac is encrypted (SQLCipher, AES-256)…". If it says "isn't encrypted yet", the copy didn't check out and MEKA keeps the plain file; the next run looks at it.

  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-07:* the deploy gap: the service stack records the commit it was deployed from (`DeployedCommit` output, `-c commit=`), and `deploy.yml` diffs against that instead of `HEAD~1`, so a server commit whose CI failed followed by an app-only fix still deploys (no recorded commit, not an ancestor, or stack unreadable → deploy; infra test added). The enrolment code is narrowed (backend `enrolWithCode`, tested in memory over HTTP and on Postgres): it never brings back a revoked device (403 `revoked`; only the server's own `enrol-device` command can) and can't start a second household (403 `household`); a device that isn't revoked can still reconnect. Both apps show the refusal in words through the core (`Enrolment.refusal`, unit-tested; the Fold and Mac already display `Failed.reason`). ADR-004 and ADR-005 amended (P-256 device keys as built, sealed key files on the Mac, the enrolment rules, the deploy rule, the leftover KMS key). CI green (run 37577176613, after a test-only fix: wire ids are `[a-z0-9]`); deployed, so the stack now records its `DeployedCommit`.
